ATHENS HIGH SCHOOL 
President Philippart called the meeting to order at 7: 00 
p.m.
In addition to Dr . Philippart, present were Board 
members Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Miletti, Schmidt, and 
Underwood.  A lso present were Superintendent 
Machesky; administrators Birmingham, Dziatczak, Griffor, 
Fulcher, Harris, Kwasny, Dixon, Jolly, West; and 
approximately 400 members of the audience.  
RECOGNITION  
Oakland County School Business 
Official of the Year – Rick L. West 
All-St
ate Athletes – Winter Sports 
and Synchronized Swim 
The Board  recognized Assistant Superintendent Rick 
West, who has been named the 2015 -16 Oakland County 
School Business Administrator of the Year. This award is 
given annually to a school business official to recognize 
outstanding accomplishments and contributions to their 
profession. Rick West was nominated by Superintendent 
Dr. Rich Machesky, with support from the district’s Board 
of Education and the entire central office staff. Trustee 
Gary Hauff attended the ceremony at Oakland Schools to 
speak on behalf of the Board. 
The judges for the award praised Mr. West’s financial 
acumen, his leadership an d his efforts to build a culture 
of trust and collaboration throughout the district. Mr. 
West is a visible and responsive member of the oneTROY 
community and has done an excellent job of steering the 
business office through challenging times. He is also an  
active member of the Michigan School Business Officials 
(MSBO), a MSBO Leadership Institute participant, and 
past president of OCSBO. 
Dr. Philippart presented Mr. West with an award from 
the Board and District and then invited OCSBO President 
Tina Kostiu k to the podium to speak and present their 
award to Mr. West. 
Mike Jolly and the Board recognized high school students 
who received the distinction of being named All- State 
athletes in their respective sports:  Monica Alluri 
(synchronized swimming) , Ellen Barker (synchronized 
swimming), Camille Bedford (synchronized swimming),

Troy middle school musicians were in Grand Rapids in 
January to perform in the 2016 Michigan All- State Band.  
These exceptional students competed against thousands 
of Michigan’s best students to earn a spot in this coveted 
ensemble.  The 3 -day experience was sponsored by the 
Michigan School Band and Orchestra Association and was 
a culmination of months of hard work and an extensive 
audition process that began last fall. 
 
 
 
Troy middle school musicians were in Grand Rapids this 
winter to perform in the 2016 Michigan All- State Honors 
Band.  These exceptional students competed against 
thousands of Michigan’s best students to earn a spot in 
this coveted ensemble.  The 3 -
day experience was 
sponsored by the Michigan School Band and Orchestra 
Association and was a culmination of months of hard 
work and an intensive audition process that began last 
fall.   
 
 
Students from across the state audition and the top 
players are  selected to perform in this prestigious 
orchestra. Students must prepare an announced piece, 
perform a series of scales and sight -read during each 10 -
minute recorded audition. The auditions are evaluated by 
an adjudicator and placed in order. The required  number 
of instruments is selected to fill each section. An average 
of over 2,000 students audition each year for the 400 
seats in our five ensembles. 
 
 
 
For more than 60 years, students across the country have 
taken up the challenge of America’s longest -running and 
most prestigious math contests, The American 
Mathematics Competitions (AMC), which is sponsored by 
the American Mathematics Society. Every year, at 
thousands of schools in every state, more than 350,000 
students are presented with a set of quest
ions rich in 
content, designed to make them think and sure to leave 
them talking.  Dedicated to strengthening the

The American Nuclear Society holds a contest every year 
that challenges middle and high school students across 
the United States to showcase their artistic talents and 
educate the public about the benefits of nuclear science 
and t
echnology.  From medical applications, electricity 
generation, and even smoke detectors, students 
submitted memes and posters about the everyday 
benefits of radiation. 
 
 
A group of experienced elementary and middle school 
teachers from all subject areas, with the help of their 
students' input, submit a variety of questions every year. 
The ideas and concepts from these questions are used to 
form the questions found in each of the six sections. The 
sections include: General Knowledge (i.e. food, art, 
sports, music, current events, entertainment, mythology, 
etc.), Language and Literature, Geography, History, 
Science, and Mathematics. The questions are not related 
to a specific curriculum, but are selected to reflect the 
general knowledge and educational levels  expected of 
sixth or eighth grade students.   
 
 
This student is the 2016 Brain Bee State Champion and 
will compete at Nationals. The Brain Bee Competition 
tests students on their neuroscience knowledge with a 
multiple choice written exam and two oral que stion and 
answer rounds. 
 
 
The 11 th Chinese Bridge Contest for U.S. high school 
students took place on March 19 th at University of 
Massachusetts in Boston.  Students already speaking 
Mandarin at home are not eligible.  The speech must be 
between 3 -4 minut es in length.  During the on- site 
contest, up to five questions will be asked immediately 
afterwards by judges, based on the delivered speech.

DECA is a business club and association of high school 
marketing students that focuses on marketing, hospitality 
and management, finance/accounting, and service.  The 
students develop public speaking skills and the ability to 
think on their feet, both of important benefits in their 
education and path to the workforce.  The three 
competitions are districts, states and international. 
Students select an area of competition such as Team 
Decision Mak ing, Individual Series, or Manuals.  The 
students must take 1 st place in their chosen category in 
order to proceed to the next level of competition. 
 
 
 
The Destination Imagination program encourages teams 
of learners to have fun, take risks, focus and frame 
challenges while incorporating STEM (science, 
technology, engineering, and mathematics), the arts and 
service of learning.  Our participants learn patience, 
flexibility, persistence, ethics, respect for others and their 
ideas, and the collaborative pro
blem solving process.  
Global Finals is the culminating event of every 
Destination Imagination season. In May, over 1,100 
teams from 45 states, 7 Canadian provinces and 13 
countries gather to showcase their Challenge solutions. 
Over 15,000 people attend Gl obal Finals to celebrate 
creativity and have fun. Students from Bemis Elementary,

These Larson students have been competing in 
Destination Imagination with Troy Creative Minds for six 
years. This year was especially exciting as they were 
invited to compete at the Destination Imagination 
European Invitational hosted in Wroclaw, Poland in April 
2016. They w ere one of only two teams from the United 
States that competed with approximately 200 Poland 
teams and 50 other teams from countries such as Turkey, 
Singapore and Qatar. The Larson team came in first place 
competing in the Improv challenge, with the highest 
score across all levels including hig h school!  In fact, they 
had a Top 10 score across all challenges and all levels 
across the 250 teams.  Way to go! 
 
 
First Lego League is a STEM based program for teams of 
up to ten kids.  Teams research a real world based 
problem and are challenged to de velop a solution.  This 
year’s challenge was “Recycling”.  They designed, built 
and programmed a robot using Lego Mindstorm.  Our 
team did research on recycling and created an app to 
make recycling expired medications much easier. 
    
 
Our team is student s driven, where students design, 
build and program competitive robots, under mentor 
supervision. In addition to building robots, students 
are trained in project management and business 
aspects, serving community and helping others. This 
has profound impact  on our students and prepares 
them for college. 
 
 
The Future Problem Solving Program provides creative 
problem solving activities for students in grades K -12.  
The skills obtained by Future Problem Solvers will last a 
lifetime and will help them in their 
academic and 
professional careers. Leadership, creativity, teamwork, 
time management, ingenuity, research skills, friendly 
competition, academic success, futuristic thinking and 
more are demonstrated and refined. The students who 
participated in the Michig an Future Problem Solving 
Program State competition, competed against over 50 
different schools across the state.  Each individual and

Michigan Arbor Day Alliance sponsors a creative writing 
contest as part of their Go Green Youth Challenge, and is 
meant to supplement the fundra
ising program by 
generating more student participation.  This contest has 
different environmental themes each year.  Michigan 
students in K -12 were asked to use their creativity and 
imagination to answer the prompt. “What Would Happen 
If Trees Were In Char ge?”  Students were urged to draw 
on personal experiences on how important trees are to 
the environment. Entries were judged on creativity, 
originality, word choice and usage, use of language, and 
appropriateness to contest theme. 
 
 
 
HOSA or “Health Occu pation Students of America” is a 
national student organization whose mission is to 
promote career opportunities in the healthcare industry. 
Students have the opportunity to compete in specific 
interest categories, including Medical Math, Forensic 
Medicine, Persuasive Speaking and Debates. There are 
more than fifty fields of interest for students to display 
their knowledge.  A record 2500 participants competed in 
the state competition in April, and the students we 
recognize here tonight all earned gold, silv er or bronze 
and qualified to compete at the National Competition in 
Tennessee.  
 
 
Detroit Tigers have sponsored this contest for 20 years.   
Contestants submit and original piece of art, essay, or 
poetry exploring one or more of Jackie Robinson’s Nine 
Values.  Essays were judged on length, creativity, rhythm 
and flow of language, organization, attitude, personality, 
character and technique used to convey meaning.

University.  There were 26 Athens students competing in

The Junior Science & Humanities Symposia at  Wayne 
State University is a national research -based competition 
sponsored by the US Army, Navy, and Air Force.  Students 
present their research to a panel of judges during the 
poster session and during a presentation.  They are able 
to share their knowled ge with other students and learn 
about future career pathways in STEM (Science, 
Technology, Engineering and Mathematics). 
 
 
This student was named a Marriott Hotels Young 
Filmmaker of the Year — he travelled to Aruba in May to 
make three commercials for the corporation. 
 
 
 
MIT Inspire is a national high school research competition 
focusing on the arts, humanities, and social sciences.  
There were 100 students from around the country that 
were selected as finalists, and they presented their 
research at MIT this past April.  This student competed in 
the literature category, entering her work titled "The 
Comparing and Contrasting of Dracula and Tess of the 
D'Urbervilles". 
 
 
National Mathcounts Michigan Team/MATHCOUNTS is a 
state and national “bee -style” comp etition for 6th - 8th 
grade students across the country and in U.S. territories 
worldwide. The competition challenges both individuals 
and teams of students with math problem -solving 
focusing on algebra, geometry and number theory. 
Students are ranked base d on their performances at 
these competitions. 
 
 
In the 1980’s, an Australian mathematician began a 
popular mathematical competition, which was open to 
anyone who wanted to participate.  The competition is a 
multiple choice test, which needs to be solved within 75 
minutes. The test includes engaging and thought-
provoking questions stemming from mathematical areas 
of knowledge including algebra, geometry, and logic. This 
year’s Math Kangaroo Olympiad took place on March 19 th

most popular events, MCWT’s web design competition, 
enables one - or two -girl teams to learn graphic

web design.   Three finalists in two categories win cash

competition.

Michigan Green Schools is a non -profit 501(c)3 agency 
dedicated to assisting all Michigan schools –  public and 
private –  achieve environmental goals which include 
protecting the air, land, water and animals of our state 
along with world outreach through good ecological 
practices and the teaching of educational stewardship of 
students pre -
kindergarten through senior high school.  
Athens High School achieved "Evergreen" status this year.

Founded in 1912, the Michigan Interscholastic Press 
Association is composed of Michigan scholastic 
journalism teachers and publication s advisers and their 
students. The organization is committed to promoting 
and recognizing excellence in journalism at all levels 
through education, training and support of student 
journalists and their advisers.  Each year, they hold a 
competition for exce llence in various categories for 
students throughout the state. The student we honor 
here tonight won first in the state for their Public Service 
Announcement about kindness.

Supporting the arts for over 40 years, the Michigan Music 
Association has been committed to providing music 
students in the State of Michigan an outlet for artistic 
talent, while helping them gain experience and

DAFT (Digital Arts, Film and Television) is proud to

been directly affected by his creativity, guidance and love

as well as at Cranbrook, Wayne State University, CCS and 
the University of Michigan.   John was also a 
cinematographer for Michael Moore's groundbreaking 
documentary, Roger and Me.”  This Athens student is the 
first recipient to ever receive the John Prusak Vi sion

The U.S. National Chemistry Olympiad (USNCO) program 
is a chemistry competition for high school students. The 
purpose of the competition is to stimulate young people 
to achieve excellence in chemistry. The American 
Chemical Society has sponsored the program since 
1984. The local competition is open to all high school 
students and the top 1000 go on to compete at the 
national level.   
 
The Twelfth National CLASS Essay Contest is a national 
Chinese essay contest sponsored by Chinese Language 
Association of Secondary -Elementary Schools. The entry 
must be student’ s original independent work. No online 
translator is allowed for creating any part of the text. The 
topic is “The Best Gift that I have ever received”.  
 
 
The National French Contest/Le Grand Concours 
(pronounced “grahn cone -coor”) is an annual 
competition sponsored by the American Association of 
Teachers of French.  Students of French in grades 1 -12, in 
all 50 states and abroad, take a written test and compete 
against students with similar educational backgrounds for 
prizes.  
 
The National German Exam is administered each year to 
high school students in the first, second, third and fourth 
level of German. The exam provides individual diagnostic 
feedback, rewards students through an extensive prize 
program, and creates a sense of accomplishment. Exam 
resu
lts provide a means of comparing students in all 
regions of the country, as well as programmatic data 
provided to help inform curricular decisions.  The exams 
are split by difficulty based on the level of German 
students have taken. The students we honor here tonight 
have all earned a National Bronze, Silver or Gold Medal 
for their performance in their respective levels.  
 
The National Language Arts League is an exam given to 
English students across the nation each year. Students 
are allotted 35 minutes t o answer 50 questi ons 
pertaining to vocabulary, grammar, spelling and reading 
comprehension.

by youth in theatre arts.  This year, more than 400

winners and nominees were selecte d from nominations

Boulan Park Middle School students earned 1 st Place in

teams from middle and high schools across the world. 
Students compete via the Internet to an swer 15 
mathematical problems, each with a non -negative 
integer answer.  
 
 
Quiz bowl is a game in which two teams compete head -
to-head to answer questions from all areas of knowledge 
including history, literature, science, fine arts, current 
events, sports , and popular culture.  The outstanding 
students we honor tonight placed first in the state at the 
Michigan Quiz Bowl Championships, which earned them 
an invitation to participate at the national level. They all 
went on to compete at the National Quiz Bowl  
Tournament for middle school students in Atlanta or the 
National Quiz Bowl Tournament for high school students 
in Dallas, where they performed very well individually 
and as a team.

The tests are self -scoring, computer contests of 100 
multiple-choice qu estions. Areas covered include math, 
geography, government, sports,  spelling, science, 
literature, English, history, general information and just 
plain fun trivia. It is meant to be a team activity with all 
around one computer. Two chances to answer correc tly 
are given. Points are awarded based on how fast they 
answer as well as on accurac y. Therefore, teams who do 
well usually are teams who can come up with a 
concensus answer quickly. An adult is needed to monitor 
but no adult help can be given. The Boulan 
Park Team 
scored 1342 out of 1500 for 1st place in the Great Lake 
State Competition.  The top five teams in each state 
competed in April, the Boulan team answered 99 of the 
100 questions correct for a score of 1338, placing them 
4th in the Nation. 
 
 
The Scholastic Awards are the nation’s largest, longest 
running, most prestigious scholarship and recognition 
program for creative teens. Si nce 1923, the Scholastic 
Awards have recognized worthy students from across the 
country. This year, the organization rec eived 320,000 
entries and the students we  honor tonight all won 
national or regional gold key medals, placing them in the 
top 1 percent in the nation. They join a legacy of 
celebrated authors and artists including Andy Warhol, 
Sylvia Plath, Truman Capote, Richard Avedon, Robert 
Redford, Lena Dunham, and the renowned filmmaker 
Ken Burns. There are 27 different categories in the 
Scholastic Art and Writing Awards and the students we 
recognize at this time won in the categories of Art, 
Critical Essay, Cinema an d Drawing.   The national gold 
medal winners also received an invitation to Carnegie 
Hall to receive the award June 2nd.  
 
 
The Science and Engineering Fair of Metropolitan Detroit 
is designed to give students in the tri -
county area an 
opportunity to devel op and exhibit science fair projects. 
The students compete for awards in multiple categories 
based on their scientific disci
pline. They present their 
STEM (Science, Technology, Engineering, and 
Mathematics) research to a panel of expert judges at the 
fair, which is held annually at Cobo Hall.

Science Olympiad competitions are comprised of 23 
individual science -related events.   These events cover a 
wide range of topics, such as chemistry, biology, physics, 
earth science, food science and engineering.   A team of 
students covers these events, usually in pairs.   They 
compete in individual tournaments and then the Oakland 
County Regional tournament. The top four teams from 
regionals qualify for the state tournament and the top 
two in the state go on to national tournaments.  Teams 
from Baker Middle School and Troy High School placed 
first in the state in individual event categories such as 
Food Science, Chemistry La b, Dynamic Planet, 
Experimental Design, Geologic Mapping and Green 
Generation. 
 
 
This student won the Oakland County Spelling Bee after 
spelling the word “submersible”.  She then advanced to 
the Super Bowl of spelling bees, the Scripps National 
Spelling Bee in Washington D.C. on May 22 nd, and placed 
11th in the nation! 
 
 
Administered by Discovery E ducation, the Siemens 
Competition encompasses the fields of science, 
technology, engineering and math.  The semifinalists are 
part of an elite nationwide group of high school students.  
They have devoted countless hours to their selected 
research projects and have achieved national recognition 
for their work. 
 
 
The Stock Market Game is a ten -week simulation of Wall 
Street trading that provides a framework for teaching 
students about the stock market, business, and economic 
decision-making and is integrated through Social Studies, 
Economics, Math, Language Arts, Technology, Life Skills, 
and Business Education.  Offered each fall and spring, the 
Stock Market Game is designed to help students 
understand the stock market.  Teams of one to three 
students compete in elementary, middle, and high school 
divisions.  Each team (or student) is given a hypothetical 
$100,000.00 to invest in common stock on the NASDAQ 
and the New York stock exchange. 
 
ExploraVision is a national science competition where 
students work in  groups to simulate real research and

The USA Biology Olympiad is a premiere biology 
competition for high school students in the United States 
that enriches the life science educ ation of nearly 10,000 
talented students annually.  The competition provides 
the motivation, curricular resources and skills training to 
take students beyond the classroom experience to the 
level of international competitiveness. 
 
 
The Verizon Innovative App Challenge  is an exciting, 
creative and collaborative competition that provides an 
opportunity for middle and high school students to apply 
knowledge about science, technology, engineering and 
math (STEM) with ingenuity and creativity to develop an 
original mobile app concept that addresses a 
need or 
problem in their school or community.  The Challenge 
shows students exciting new possibilities for their 
futures, opening doors they may never have known were 
available.

President Philippart began the meeting at 7:00 p.m. with a 
public hearing for the 2016-2017 proposed budget.  Mr. West 
presented the details of the proposed budget to the Board 
and community and shared a PowerPoint presentation.  He 
shared that the proposed budget was reviewed in great detail 
with the Board at the June 14
th  workshop.  He talked about 
tax values and homestead/non-homestead forecasts.  He 
talked about the foundation allowance and the increase in 
student enrollment.  He also reviewed general fund revenues 
and expenditures.  
 
There was brief discussion amongst Board members then Dr. 
Philippart invited members of the audience to ask questions 
or share comments.   No members of the audience addressed 
the Board with comments or questions so president Philippart 
closed the public hearing at 7:18 p.m. and moved on to the 
regular meeting.  
 
  
In addition to Dr. Philippart, present were Board members 
Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Miletti, and Schmidt.  Ms. 
Underwood was not in attendance. Also present were 
Superintendent Machesky; Administrators Birmingham, 
Dziatczak, Griffor, Harris, Kwasny, Silagy, and West; and 
approximately 60 additional members of the audience. 
 
 
 
Tonight the District and Board honored Ed Morykwas, 
photographer/photojournalist/webmaster of School Life Troy.  
Ed has become an integral part of the Troy School District, 
going above and beyond the call of duty to help showcase our 
students and staff and tell our stories. He will be retiring at 
the end of the month. Ed helped to launch School Life two 
years ago and in that time, became a regular fixture in every 
school, at every event and in every celebration. With his

Wayne State University's Center for Peace and Conflict 
Studies canvassed the Detroit metropolitan area for 
educators to study abroad for their educational program 
“Discovering Japan in the 21 
st  Century: Reconciliation with the 
Past”.  Ten educators were chosen to participate in the 
project – Mrs. Mori was one of them.  She guided 11 non-
Japanese speaking educators throughout the entire tour and 
made the trip a great success!  Mrs. Mori went above and 
beyond her duties to assist seven teachers and two board of 
education members for ten days in Japan. Ms. Brenda Carter, 
President-Elect of the Michigan Association of School Boards, 
asked to join the Board this evening to recognize and honor 
Mrs. Mori for her contributions to their project.   Mrs. Carter 
was invited to come up to say a few words and present Mrs. 
Mori with her certificate.

The Board congratulated Dr. Machesky for being named a 
2016 “Superintendent to Watch” by the National Schools 
Public Relations Association. This prestigious program 
recognizes less than 25 superintendents across the nation for 
their dynamic, fast-paced leadership with strong 
communication at its core. The leaders honored in this way 
engage and inform their school communities with new 
communication technology tools combined with tried-and-
true techniques. The judges praised Dr. Machesky’s 
commitment to effective communication and his 
understanding that it leads to school and student success. 
They were impressed by his willingness to expand two-way 
communication and outreach efforts and his OneTROY 
message of unity, value and inclusion of all.  Dr. Machesky is 
one of a select group to receive this honor this year. He was 
previously named the Michigan Outstanding Superintendent 
Communicator of the Year and will be formally named with 
this new recognition at NSPRA’s national conference in 
Chicago in July.

TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION RESOLUTION URGING THE 
GOVERNOR TO VETO APPROPRIATION LEGISLATION FOR 
NONPUBLIC SCHOOLS WITHIN THE STATE OF MICHIGAN 
 
It was moved by Mr. Schmidt and supported by Mrs. Edmunds 
that; 
 
WHEREAS,  the Michigan Legislature has proposed a School 
Aid Budget which allocates $2.5 million for nonpublic schools. 
 
WHEREAS  these funds have been specifically allocated to 
nonpublic schools as “reimbursements” for state-mandated 
39
  7 June 21, 2016 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Board Comments  
conduct. 
WHEREAS  these funds could be utilized to “reimburse” 
nonpublic schools for such things as the costs associated with 
employing staff, including noncertified teachers, as well as the 
provision of mentor teachers, and the expenses associated 
with certain specific areas of instruction, such as government 
and history, among other things. 
 
WHEREAS,  Article VIII Sec. 2 of the Michigan Constitution 
specifically prohibits: 
 
- the use of public money to aid or maintain a nonpublic  
school; 
- the use of public money to support the attendance of any 
student at a nonpublic school; and 
- the use of public money to employ an individual at a 
nonpublic school. 
 
WHEREAS  this appropriation is likely a violation of the 
Michigan Constitution. 
 
 
WHEREAS public schools do not receive similar additional 
funds specifically allocated to reimburse those schools for the 
costs of these same basic purposes.  
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED  that the Troy Board of 
Education urges the Governor of the State of Michigan to 
VETO Section 152b of the Legislature’s proposed School Aid 
Budget and thereby uphold the Michigan Constitution as well 
as the will of the people, as demonstrated by multiple prior 
ballot initiatives to fund private schools with public funds, all 
of which were rejected by the citizens of this state.   
 
Yes:            6 
No:             0 
Absent:      Underwood

   7. A.      07.19.16 
 
 
7. CURRICULUM 
 
 A.  CONSIDERATION:    Michigan High School Athletic Association 
                                               (MHSAA) Membership 
 
To continue membership in the Michigan High School Athletic Association (a 
voluntary, nonprofit corporation), it is necessary for the Troy Board of Education to 
adopt the following resolution for our middle schools and high schools.  This 
membership for the year August 1, 2016, through July 31, 2017, will enable our 
schools to participate  in MHSAA-sponsored tournaments and meets in the various 
sports. 
 
     
 
SUGGESTED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
 
  Moved by  
 
  Supported by  
 
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the six (6) secondary schools  which are 
under the direction of this Bo ard of Education/Governing Body of  the Troy School 
District, City of Troy, County of Oakland, of the state of Michigan, are hereby: 
 
(A) enrolled as members of the Michigan High School Athletic Association, Inc., a 
nonprofit association, and 
(B) are further enrolled to participate in the approved interschool athletic  
 activities sponsored by said association. 
 
The Board of Education/Governing Body hereby delegates to the Superintendent or 
his/her designee(s) the responsibility for the supervision and control of said activities 
and hereby accepts the Constitution and By -Laws of said association and adopts as 
its own the rules, regulations and interpretations (as minimum standards) as 
published in the current HANDBOOK as the governing c ode under which the sai d 
school(s) shall conduct its program of interscholastic athletics and agrees to primary 
enforcement of said rules, regulations, interpretations and qualifications.  In 
addition, it is hereby agreed that schools which host or participate in the 
association’s meets and tournaments shall follow and enforce all tournament 
policies, procedures and schedules. 
 
This authorization shall be effective from August 1, 2016, and shall remain effective 
until July 31, 2017, during which the authorization may not be revoked.

7. CURRICULUM 
 
 B.  CONSIDERATION:    High School Cooperative Team Agreement  
 
The District is  seeking approval  from the Michigan High School Athletic 
Association (MHSAA) to operate a cooperative team in Boys’ and Girls’ Skiing 
with Athens High School.  It would be appropriate for the Board of Education 
to consider passing the following resolution at this time.  A copy of the 
resolution will be presented with our application to the MHSAA for approval. 
 
     
 
SUGGESTED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
 
  Moved by  
 
  Supported by  
 
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that Athens High School agree to join together 
to fund,  support and maintain a  cooperative team in the sport of Boys’ and 
Girls’ Skiing for the 2016-17 and 2017-18 school years.
